# Chapter 10 — The Pelvic Floor: Kegels, Reverse Kegels & Beyond

The pelvic floor is the only part of the male sexual system that's both directly involved in erection, ejaculation, and orgasm *and* under voluntary control. Training it — in both directions, contraction and release — is the physical foundation for arousal control, orgasm intensity, ejaculatory force, and the advanced practices in Chapter 12. It's also an evidence-based treatment for premature ejaculation and a contributor to erectile function. This chapter is the training manual.

## 10.1 What the pelvic floor does during sex

Review from Chapter 2, with function emphasized:

| Muscle | Sexual function | Trained how |
|---|---|---|
| **Bulbospongiosus** | Rhythmic contractions expel semen; compresses the bulb to increase glans engorgement; its contractions are much of what you *feel* as orgasm | Kegels (the "push out the last drop of urine" squeeze) |
| **Ischiocavernosus** | Compresses the roots of the penis, increasing rigidity ("flexing" the erection) | Kegels (the "lift the erection" squeeze) |
| **Pubococcygeus / levator ani** | Supports pelvic organs; involved in the sense of "urgency" before ejaculation; its involuntary contraction is the 8.5–9 signal | Kegels and, critically, reverse Kegels |
| **External anal sphincter** | Co-contracts with the rest; anal contractions during orgasm | Same |
| **External urethral sphincter** | Closes during emission; opens during expulsion | Same |

**Erection:** a strong ischiocavernosus/bulbospongiosus system improves rigidity and helps maintain erection by compressing venous outflow. In a randomized trial, 40% of men with ED regained normal function and a further 35% improved after 3 months of pelvic floor exercises plus lifestyle advice [Dorey 2005].

**Ejaculatory control:** in lifelong PE, 12 weeks of pelvic floor rehabilitation improved control in 82.5% of men and increased latency from ~32 seconds to ~146 seconds on average [Pastore 2014]. Longer-term follow-up found roughly half maintained the benefit at several years [Pastore 2018]. The mechanism is thought to be both strength (ability to inhibit the expulsion reflex) and, importantly, *awareness* — men learn to feel what the pelvic floor is doing and to relax it.

**Orgasm intensity and ejaculatory force:** anecdotally near-universal; mechanistically obvious (stronger, better-coordinated muscles → stronger contractions → more forceful expulsion and more intense sensation). Controlled data are sparse, but this is one of the most consistently reported effects of pelvic floor training among men.

## 10.2 Finding the muscles

You need to isolate the pelvic floor from the abdominals, buttocks, and thighs — most beginners squeeze everything.

**Method 1 — Urine stop.** While urinating, stop the stream mid-flow. The muscles you used are the pelvic floor. Don't do this regularly as an exercise — it can interfere with bladder function — just once or twice to identify the sensation.

**Method 2 — The lift.** Sitting or lying, imagine lifting your testicles toward your body without using your hands or abs. Or imagine drawing the perineum upward.

**Method 3 — Anal squeeze.** Squeeze as if stopping gas. The anus tightens and lifts. This recruits the posterior pelvic floor; combine with the lift for the full group.

**Method 4 — Erect check.** With an erection, make it twitch upward. That's the ischiocavernosus/bulbospongiosus — the anterior pelvic floor.

**Method 5 — Palpation.** Place a finger on the perineum (between scrotum and anus). Contract. You should feel the tissue lift and firm. Release; it should drop and soften.

**Checks for correct isolation:**
- Abdomen stays soft (hand on it).
- Buttocks don't clench (you don't rise off the chair).
- Thighs relaxed.
- Breathing normally — not holding breath.
- Nobody watching could tell.

If you can't isolate it, that's common. Start with the anal squeeze — easiest to feel — and build from there. A pelvic floor physiotherapist can teach it in one session.

## 10.3 Two directions: contraction and release

Almost every online Kegel guide teaches only contraction. This is a mistake with consequences.

**The hypertonic pelvic floor.** Many men — especially anxious men, men who sit all day, cyclists, men with a history of pelvic pain, and men who've done years of Kegels without release training — have a pelvic floor that is *chronically tight*, not weak. Symptoms: premature ejaculation, pelvic or perineal pain, pain after ejaculation, urinary hesitancy or frequency, erectile difficulty (a tight floor can compress arteries and nerves), constipation, and difficulty relaxing at high arousal. Chronic prostatitis/chronic pelvic pain syndrome is increasingly understood, in many cases, as a pelvic floor tension disorder [Anderson 2005].

For these men, more Kegels make things worse. What they need is **release**.

**The reverse Kegel** is the deliberate relaxation and gentle downward push of the pelvic floor — the sensation of beginning to urinate or gently bearing down. The perineum drops; the anus softens and opens slightly; the base of the penis relaxes.

A healthy pelvic floor can **contract strongly, hold, release completely, and relax at rest.** Both directions. The goal is range and control, not just strength.

## 10.4 The training program

### Phase 1 — Awareness (weeks 1–2)

Daily, 5 minutes, lying down with knees bent.

1. **Breathe** slowly. Notice the pelvic floor moving with breath: it drops slightly on inhale, rises on exhale. Diaphragm and pelvic floor move together.
2. **Contract** gently (30–50% effort) on the exhale. Hold 3 seconds. Focus on *what it feels like*.
3. **Release** on the inhale. Feel it drop. Then let it drop *further* — the reverse Kegel. Hold 3 seconds.
4. Repeat 10 times.
5. Finish with 1 minute of complete relaxation, attention on the perineum feeling soft and heavy.

The goal is the map, not strength.

### Phase 2 — Strength and endurance (weeks 3–8)

Daily, 8–10 minutes. Sitting, standing, or lying; standing is hardest.

**Set A — Quick flicks.** 20 rapid contract-release cycles, ~1/second, full release between. Trains fast-twitch fibers used in expulsion and the quick erection "lift."

**Set B — Holds.** 10 contractions held 5 seconds, 5 seconds full release between. Progress to 10-second holds. Trains the endurance needed to maintain inhibition at high arousal.

**Set C — The elevator.** Contract to 25%, hold 2s; 50%, 2s; 75%, 2s; 100%, 2s; then down 75, 50, 25, release. 2–3 cycles. Trains fine control — partial contraction is what makes the pelvic floor a usable throttle during edging.

**Set D — Reverse Kegels.** 10 deliberate releases, each held 5–10 seconds, normal breath between. Focus on the perineum dropping and anus softening. *Not optional.*

**Set E — Erect practice (2–3×/week).** With an erection: 10 slow contractions feeling the penis lift; 10 flicks; 5 reverse Kegels feeling the base soften. Builds the link between exercise and sexual use.

**Progression:** longer holds, more reps, standing. Don't exceed ~15 minutes/day total.

### Phase 3 — Integration (ongoing)

The pelvic floor becomes a tool used *during* sessions:

- **Arousal pulsing (6–7).** During edging pauses, rhythmic contractions maintain or raise arousal without touch. Many men feel a distinct wave of sensation with each contraction at moderate arousal.
- **Reverse Kegel at the edge (8.5–9).** As involuntary pelvic tension begins, consciously release and bear down gently. For most trained men this drops arousal reliably — the single most useful edging tool.
- **Flex during orgasm.** Strong contractions timed with the involuntary ones intensify them and increase ejaculatory force.
- **Release during orgasm.** Alternatively, deliberately relaxing at orgasm lets the sensation spread and lengthens it for some men. Try both.
- **The clench for non-ejaculatory orgasm.** Chapter 12 — a sustained strong contraction at 9.5 can, with practice, block or reduce ejaculation while allowing orgasm. Not for everyone; has caveats.
- **Erection maintenance.** When the erection softens mid-session, a series of contractions restores rigidity by compressing venous outflow.
- **Post-orgasm.** Gentle contractions expel remaining semen.

## 10.5 Relaxation training for the tight pelvic floor

If you have signs of hypertonicity (PE, pelvic pain, urinary hesitancy, pain after ejaculation, difficulty with reverse Kegels, a perineum that feels hard or tender), emphasize release:

- **Diaphragmatic breathing**, 5 minutes daily, lying down, hand on belly, letting the belly rise and pelvic floor drop on each inhale.
- **Reverse Kegels** as the primary exercise; contractions only at low intensity for awareness.
- **Stretches**: deep squat (heels on a rolled towel if needed), happy baby, child's pose, butterfly, pigeon, hip-flexor lunges. 1–2 minutes each, slow breathing. Tight hips = tight floor.
- **Perineal self-massage.** Lubricated thumb, firm slow pressure on the perineum, find tender spots, hold 30–60 seconds until they soften. A soft massage ball under the perineum while sitting (gently) also works.
- **Heat.** Warm bath; heating pad on perineum/lower abdomen.
- **Internal release.** Advanced; best learned from a pelvic floor physiotherapist: a lubricated finger in the anus pressing on tight levator bands (sideways/backward, not toward the prostate) with sustained pressure. Therapeutic wands exist for this.
- **Reduce contributors**: prolonged sitting (move hourly), cycling without a cut-out saddle, chronic stress, holding urine, habitual clenching (check during the day — is your floor gripping while you work?).

A pelvic floor physiotherapist — yes, they treat men — is the best investment if symptoms are significant.

## 10.6 Overtraining and common errors

- **Too much.** 10–15 minutes daily is plenty. Hundreds of Kegels a day produces hypertonicity, pain, and *worse* control. Sore or tight → cut back, emphasize release.
- **Holding breath.** Creates intra-abdominal pressure pushing *down* on the floor. Always breathe.
- **Bearing down instead of lifting.** Check with a finger: contraction lifts, bearing down bulges.
- **Recruiting abs, glutes, thighs.** Use gentler effort until isolated.
- **Only contracting, never releasing.** Half the training is release.
- **Expecting results in a week.** Neuromuscular changes take 4–6 weeks; strength 8–12. Consistency beats intensity.
- **Urine-stopping as regular practice.** Identification only.

## 10.7 Devices and adjuncts

- **Biofeedback** (probe + display) is used in physiotherapy and is highly effective for men who can't feel the muscles.
- **Electrical stimulation** for very weak floors; rarely needed.
- **Interval timer apps** help adherence.
- **Prostate massagers** (Chapter 13) give feedback and resistance for internal awareness — contracting around the toy is itself an exercise.

## 10.8 The pelvic floor and posture

Anterior pelvic tilt (excess lower-back arch, common in sitters) shortens and tightens the floor. Slouching can weaken it. Neutral pelvis — weight through the sitting bones, level pelvis — lets the floor rest at optimal length. Daytime awareness plus hip mobility work supports everything else here.

## 10.9 A 12-week summary program

| Weeks | Focus | Daily work |
|---|---|---|
| 1–2 | Awareness | 5 min: breath-linked gentle contractions and releases, lying down |
| 3–4 | Foundation | 8 min: 20 flicks, 10×5s holds, 10 reverse Kegels; elevator 2× |
| 5–6 | Build | 10 min: 20 flicks, 10×7s holds, 3 elevators, 10 reverse Kegels; some standing; erect practice 2×/wk |
| 7–8 | Strength | 12 min: 30 flicks, 10×10s holds, 3 elevators, 10 reverse Kegels; standing; erect practice 3×/wk |
| 9–10 | Integration | Continue 10 min; use in sessions: arousal pulsing, reverse Kegel at the edge |
| 11–12 | Refinement | Maintenance 8–10 min; experiment with flex vs. release at orgasm |
| Ongoing | Maintenance | 5–10 min, 4–5×/week; release work always included |

Track: how close to 9 you can hold, erection firmness, orgasm intensity (1–10), ejaculatory force, any pain or tightness.

---

### Key takeaways

- The pelvic floor is the **voluntary control panel** for erection rigidity, ejaculatory timing, and orgasm intensity.
- Evidence: training improves **erectile function** [Dorey 2005] and gives **ejaculatory control** to ~80% of men with lifelong PE [Pastore 2014].
- **Find it**: lift the testicles, stop the stream, squeeze the anus, twitch the erection. Isolate. Breathe.
- **Train both directions.** A **tight floor** is as common as a weak one and causes PE, pain, and ED. With those symptoms, emphasize release.
- **Program**: flicks, holds, elevators, reverse Kegels, erect practice. 10–15 min/day, 12 weeks. Don't overtrain.
- **In sessions**: pulse at 6–7 to build; **reverse Kegel at 9** to back off; flex or release at orgasm to shape it.
- **Pelvic floor physiotherapists treat men.**

# Chapter 11 — Pleasure Maximization: The Science and Craft of Feeling More

Everything before this chapter was infrastructure. This chapter is about the goal: the deliberate engineering of the most pleasurable experience your body can produce. We approach it as a design problem — what are the inputs to pleasure, how does each one work, and how do you combine them?

The short version: pleasure is not one thing. It's the sum of at least six separable systems — anticipation, sensation, arousal state, attention, novelty, and orgasm mechanics — each of which most men leave at default settings. Turning each one up, and learning how they interact, is what this chapter teaches.

## 11.1 A model of sexual pleasure

### The two systems: wanting and liking

Neuroscience distinguishes two pleasure-related systems [Berridge & Kringelbach 2015]:

- **Wanting (incentive salience)** — dopamine-driven. The pull toward a reward; anticipation; craving; the intensifying "I need this" state. Active in the build-up and peaks just before the reward.
- **Liking (hedonic impact)** — opioid- and endocannabinoid-driven. The actual pleasure of the reward when it arrives. Concentrated at orgasm and in the afterglow.

These are separable. Wanting can be intense without much liking (compulsive behavior is exactly that pattern). Liking can occur without much wanting (a pleasant surprise). The best sexual experiences maximize *both* — a long, intensifying wanting phase that resolves into a strong liking phase.

**Practical consequence:** the wanting phase is *itself* pleasurable — hugely so. The rising, teasing, almost-there state at arousal 8–9 is one of the most intensely enjoyable states the body can produce, and it can be sustained for a long time. Most men treat it as an obstacle between them and the orgasm. Skilled men treat it as the main course.

### Six inputs

| Input | What it is | Default in most men | Ceiling |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Anticipation** | The wanting build-up; teasing, delay, denial | Nearly zero (touch → orgasm as fast as possible) | Hours of building desire before a session; extended edging |
| **Sensation** | The raw sensory signal — quantity, quality, variety of stimulation | Single zone (penis), single technique | Multiple zones, layered techniques, temperature, texture, vibration |
| **Arousal state** | The physiological readiness of the body — parasympathetic depth, blood flow, hormonal state | Rushed, tense, distracted | Deeply relaxed, warm, unhurried, fully engorged |
| **Attention** | How much of the sensation actually gets processed | Mostly on a screen or the future | Fully on the body, immersed |
| **Novelty** | Difference from what the nervous system expects | None — same routine | Deliberate variation on every axis |
| **Orgasm mechanics** | Pelvic floor state, build-up duration, stimulation at the moment of orgasm | Whatever happens | Engineered for duration and spread |

Each of these is a dial. The rest of the chapter goes through them.

## 11.2 Anticipation

Dopamine responds to *predicted* reward, and the response is highest when the reward is near but not yet delivered. This is why the near-miss is so pleasurable, why teasing works, and why a session you've been looking forward to all day is better than one you started on impulse.

### Techniques

**Pre-session anticipation.**
- Decide in the morning that you'll have a session tonight. Notice how anticipation shapes the day.
- Deliberately abstain for 2–4 days before a planned "big" session. Seminal vesicle filling increases, sensitivity increases, and wanting accumulates. (Beyond about a week the returns diminish and sensation may actually dull for some men.)
- Build a ritual (Chapter 8) that signals "it's coming" — the shower, the lighting, the transition. The ritual itself becomes a dopamine trigger.
- Low-level arousal during the day — brief thoughts, a moment of noticing your body — without acting on it. Arrive at the session already at a 3.

**In-session teasing.**
- Delay genital contact. Five to ten minutes of non-genital touch (thighs, abdomen, chest, scrotum, perineum) while the penis is untouched. The erection you get from *not* being touched is a different quality.
- Approach and retreat. Hand moving toward the penis, brushing past, moving away. Repeated. This is what a skilled partner does; you can do it to yourself.
- Light, incomplete strokes. Touch that's obviously less than you want. Fingertips instead of a grip. Sustain it long past the point where you want more.
- Rule-setting. "No glans contact for the first fifteen minutes." "Only the non-dominant hand until arousal 7." Arbitrary constraints create anticipation for their release.

**Edging** (Chapter 9) is anticipation engineering at the top of the arousal scale — repeatedly bringing the reward within reach and withdrawing it. Every near-miss spikes dopamine. Ten near-misses is ten spikes. The eventual orgasm arrives on a wave of accumulated wanting.

**Denial and extended arousal.** Some men practice sessions with no orgasm at all, or several sessions across days without orgasm, ending with a single orgasm. The anticipation builds across days rather than minutes. Experiences are reported as extremely intense. The cost is prostatic/testicular congestion (mild ache; resolves with ejaculation) and, for some men, irritability or distraction. It's a legitimate tool used occasionally; not a lifestyle recommendation.

## 11.3 Sensation: more, different, layered

### Quantity: more zones

Chapter 2 mapped the erogenous zones. The penis is one input channel. Adding others doesn't just add pleasure — sensory inputs from different zones **summate** in the spinal cord and brain, so simultaneous stimulation of two zones can produce more than twice the arousal of either alone.

**Zones to add, roughly in order of how much most men get from them:**

1. **Scrotum.** Cupping, gentle tugging (especially downward, which also delays ejaculation), light scratching, stroking the raphe, rolling the testes gently. Warmth from the palm.
2. **Perineum.** Firm rhythmic pressure with fingertips or knuckles, especially at 8–9 and during orgasm. Stimulates the bulb and (indirectly) the prostate.
3. **Nipples.** Circling, pinching, flicking, pulling. Highly variable — some men feel nothing at first and develop sensitivity with practice over weeks; others are immediately responsive. Nipple sensitivity in men appears to be partly a *learned* connection; regular stimulation during high arousal builds it.
4. **Inner thighs and groin crease.** Light touch; the "almost there" zones adjacent to the genitals. Pure anticipation triggers.
5. **Anus (external).** Circling, light pressure, without penetration. Nerve-dense. Many men who'd never consider internal play find external anal touch adds a distinct layer.
6. **Prostate (internal).** Chapter 12. The single largest addition available.
7. **Lower abdomen, just above the pubic bone.** Pressure here compresses the base of the penis/bladder area and can intensify sensation at high arousal.
8. **Frenulum-specific attention.** Not a new zone, but most men stroke *past* the frenulum rather than *on* it. Dedicated fingertip attention to the frenulum while the other hand does something else is a major upgrade.
9. **Glans edge (corona) with fingernail or textured object.** Very light. Novel.
10. **Neck, ears, scalp, lower back, buttocks, feet.** Individual. Worth mapping once.

### The mapping exercise

One session, no orgasm goal, 30 minutes. Systematically touch every zone above with three types of touch (light fingertip, firm pressure, moving stroke) for 30 seconds each, at moderate arousal (6–7), and rate each 0–5. Write it down (Chapter 21 template). You will discover zones you didn't know you had, and you'll know which ones to layer into future sessions. Repeat the mapping every few months — sensitivity changes with practice.

### Quality: types of stimulation

Skin has distinct receptor types. Meissner corpuscles respond to light touch and low-frequency vibration; Pacinian corpuscles to deep pressure and high-frequency vibration (around 200–300 Hz); Merkel discs to sustained pressure and texture; Ruffini endings to stretch; free nerve endings to temperature and light "tickle" touch. Different techniques recruit different receptors, and **recruiting more receptor types produces richer sensation**.

- **Stroke** (friction, moving pressure) — Meissner + Merkel.
- **Squeeze / static pressure** — Merkel + Pacinian.
- **Stretch / traction** — Ruffini. Pulling the skin, gentle traction on the shaft or scrotum.
- **Vibration** — Pacinian (and Meissner at low frequencies). A vibrator on the frenulum or glans recruits receptors that hands can't reach effectively. This is why vibration feels categorically different, not just "more."
- **Temperature** — free nerve endings. Warm hands vs. cool; warmed lube vs. cool lube; a warm cloth on the scrotum; a cool object briefly on the glans; alternating. Temperature contrast is one of the most underused sensation multipliers.
- **Texture** — Merkel. Silk, fleece, a textured sleeve, a soft brush, the ridges of your own fingerprints at glacial speed.
- **Tickle / feather-light** — free nerve endings and C-tactile afferents (the "pleasant touch" fibers that respond to slow, light stroking at ~3–10 cm/s, the speed of a caress). This is the neuroscience behind why very slow, light stroking of the inner thigh or shaft feels *emotionally* pleasant, not just sexually — it's the affiliative touch system.

**Design principle:** in a single session, aim to recruit at least three receptor types. Stroke + temperature + stretch. Or vibration + light tickle + pressure. Never just one.

### Layering: simultaneous and sequential

- **Simultaneous.** Two hands, two zones: penis + scrotum; penis + perineum; penis + nipple; penis + anus. Or a toy on one zone (vibrator on frenulum) while a hand does another. The summation effect is real and large.
- **Sequential.** Alternating zones every 30–60 seconds. Prevents habituation in any one zone (see 11.5). Penis → scrotum → perineum → penis. Each return to the penis feels fresh.
- **Contrasting.** Firm on one zone, feather-light on another. Warm on one, cool on another. Contrast amplifies both.

## 11.4 Arousal state: the body's readiness

The same touch on a tense, cold, distracted body and a warm, relaxed, absorbed body produces different sensation — not slightly different; categorically. Chapter 8 covered the setting; here, the physiological levers specifically.

- **Warmth.** Blood flow to the skin and genitals is higher when warm. Scrotal skin is more sensitive when relaxed (warm) than retracted (cold). A warm room, a warm shower beforehand, a blanket. Not trivial.
- **Time since last orgasm.** 2–4 days for most men produces peak sensitivity and volume. Daily orgasm is fine but each one is somewhat less intense. Very long gaps (>1–2 weeks) sometimes *reduce* sensitivity paradoxically for some men, and increase the risk of a fast, uncontrolled orgasm. For a maximum-intensity session, 3 days is a common sweet spot.
- **Circadian timing.** Testosterone peaks in the early morning. Many men report morning sessions produce firmer erections and more intense sensation, though evening is more common for convenience. Try a weekend morning.
- **Post-exercise.** 30–90 minutes after moderate exercise, blood flow is elevated, endorphins are up, and many men report heightened sensation. Immediately after exhausting exercise is worse (fatigue, sympathetic dominance).
- **Hydration and nutrition.** Dehydration reduces ejaculate volume and can reduce sensation. A heavy meal diverts blood. Moderate: hydrated, not full.
- **Parasympathetic depth.** The 8.4 transition ritual. Ten slow breaths and a body scan genuinely change what you'll feel in the next 30 minutes.
- **Prior arousal.** A session that begins with low-level arousal already present (from anticipation, from a few minutes of erotic material earlier, from having been "on the edge" of interest for hours) reaches deeper states than one that starts from zero.
- **Sleep.** Rested men have better erections, more sensation, and more control. Late-night exhausted sessions are the worst version of the experience.
- **Substances.** Alcohol beyond one drink reduces sensation and erection. Cannabis at low doses enhances sensation and time-dilation for many men (and impairs at higher doses). Caffeine excess raises sympathetic tone. Nicotine constricts blood vessels. Chapter 18.

## 11.5 Novelty and habituation

The nervous system is a change detector. A constant stimulus fades from awareness — you don't feel your clothes. This applies to sexual stimulation on every timescale:

- **Within a stroke:** a constant-speed, constant-pressure stroke registers less than one that varies.
- **Within a session:** ten minutes of the same technique produces less and less sensation per stroke.
- **Across sessions:** the same routine every time, year after year, flattens.
- **Across erotic material:** the same content, or the same genre, loses its effect; the search for novelty in content is the mechanism behind escalation (Chapter 14).

**Countermeasures:**

- **Micro-variation.** Within each stroke, vary something: the pressure peaks at the corona and lightens on the shaft; the speed decelerates at the top; the grip rotates slightly. A stroke with internal structure is felt more than a uniform one.
- **Change every 60–90 seconds.** Grip, zone, speed, motion, hand. Before the current technique fades. Cycle through your repertoire (Chapter 6's inventory).
- **Surprise yourself.** Irregular rhythm — three slow, one fast; a pause; a sudden light touch somewhere else. Predictability is habituation's ally.
- **Session-to-session variety.** Don't use the same structure twice in a row. Alternate: hand-only / toy; visual material / eyes closed; long / short; morning / evening; supine / kneeling; ejaculatory / non-ejaculatory.
- **The occasional fast.** A few days off completely, then a session. Sensitivity rebounds.
- **Break the "finishing move."** Most men have one technique they always use to orgasm. That technique gets conditioned as *the* orgasm trigger and everything else becomes preamble. Deliberately finish with a different technique every session for a month. This is also therapeutic for narrow conditioning (Chapter 16).
- **Novelty in the wanting system.** New fantasy, new material, new scenario. Powerful but with the escalation caveat — Chapter 14 discusses using novelty in content sustainably.

**Important nuance:** novelty and *depth* are in tension. Constant change prevents habituation but also prevents the immersion that comes from sustained attention to one sensation. The skilled approach: **novelty in the build-up, depth at the peak.** Vary widely through arousal 4–8; then, in the 8–9 zone, settle into a single technique and go deep on it with full attention.

## 11.6 Attention: getting the signal to the brain

Chapter 8 made the argument: pleasure ≈ sensation × attention. Here's how to maximize the attention term during high arousal specifically.

- **Eyes closed at the peak.** Whatever your relationship with visual material, close your eyes from 8 onward. Visual processing consumes an enormous share of cortical resources. Closing the eyes releases it to somatosensory processing.
- **Narrow the field, then widen it.** At 8, put attention on one square centimeter — the frenulum, say. Feel everything there. Then deliberately widen: the whole penis. Then the whole pelvis. Then the whole body. This narrow-wide oscillation deepens immersion and is the beginning of "whole-body" experience.
- **Track the wave.** Attention on arousal *itself* — its rising, its cresting, its receding — rather than on the stimulus producing it. This is what makes edging immersive rather than mechanical.
- **Breath as anchor.** When attention wanders, return via the breath. One long exhale. Back to the body.
- **Sound.** Vocalizing — a low hum, a sigh, a groan on the exhale — does several things: extends the exhale (parasympathetic), relaxes the jaw and throat (reflexively linked to pelvic floor relaxation), gives attention an anchor, and deepens immersion. If privacy permits, use it. Men who are habitually silent often find that allowing sound noticeably changes the experience.
- **Movement.** Rocking the pelvis, arching, small thrusts, tensing and releasing the legs. Movement engages the motor system in the experience, prevents the frozen-tense posture, and — in the case of pelvic movement — adds sensation from the hip and pelvic floor muscles. Stillness is appropriate for mindfulness practice; for peak pleasure, most men do better moving.
- **Remove the clock.** Even a glance at the time drops arousal. Alarm set, clock hidden.
- **Immersion aids.** Headphones (with or without audio), a blindfold, darkness. Sensory reduction in the non-tactile channels raises the salience of the tactile one.

## 11.7 Orgasm mechanics: engineering the peak

The orgasm itself can be shaped. Variables:

### Build-up duration

The longer the plateau, the more intense the orgasm — up to a point. Mechanisms: seminal vesicle and prostate filling (more volume, more forceful expulsion); pelvic floor priming; neurochemical accumulation; and, simply, having been present and aroused for longer so the release is larger. Most men find the curve rises steeply from 5 to 20 minutes of plateau, continues rising to about 45–60 minutes, and flattens or declines after that as fatigue and skin sensitivity set in.

### Number of near-misses

Each edge cycle adds to the eventual orgasm. Anecdotally, the relationship is roughly linear up to 5–10 cycles, then flattens. Beyond ~15–20, some men report the orgasm actually becomes *less* intense (fatigue, "overcooking"). Find your number.

### Pelvic floor state at orgasm

- **Contracting hard** at the first involuntary pulse: more forceful ejaculation, sharper and more localized sensation, often shorter.
- **Fully relaxed** (reverse Kegel maintained through orgasm): less forceful ejaculation, more diffuse spreading sensation, often longer, sometimes with fewer distinct contractions but a longer overall "wave."
- **Alternating** — flex on the first few contractions, then release: some men report this gets the best of both.
- **The clench** (sustained maximum contraction from 9.5): may block ejaculation partly or fully — Chapter 12.

### Stimulation during orgasm

- **Continue the same stroke** through all the contractions: the "standard."
- **Stop at the first contraction**: the orgasm continues on its own; some men find this makes the internal sensation clearer and longer without the "overload" of continued glans stimulation. Others find it unsatisfying. Try it.
- **Switch to the frenulum** with light fingertip pressure at the first contraction: intensifies for many.
- **Add perineal pressure** — firm, rhythmic, timed with contractions: this is one of the most reliable intensifiers. The other hand's knuckles pressing upward on the perineum in time with the pulses.
- **Add prostate stimulation** (Chapter 12): the largest intensifier available, for men who've developed it.
- **Add scrotal traction** — gently pulling down during orgasm: some men report this lengthens the contractions.
- **Slow down** rather than speed up at 9: a slow, light approach to orgasm produces a "rolling" rather than "explosive" quality. Both are good; they're different.

### Breath at orgasm

Most men hold their breath at orgasm. Try the opposite: a long, slow exhale beginning at 9.5 and continuing through the contractions, with sound if possible. Many men find this transforms the experience — the orgasm spreads through the body rather than staying locked in the pelvis, and lasts longer.

### Body position at orgasm

Standing or kneeling engages more muscle and often produces a more intense (though sometimes shorter) orgasm than lying flat. Arching the back and pushing the pelvis forward at orgasm is a common instinct — follow it, or experiment with the opposite (curling forward, which compresses the perineum).

### Attention at orgasm

Stay. Don't check out. Don't reach for tissues. Attention on the contractions, the sensation in the pelvis, the spreading warmth, the breath. The orgasm is 10–20 seconds; men who are fully present for all of it report it as much longer and more intense than men who "come to" halfway through.

### Post-orgasm: the afterglow

Prolactin, oxytocin, endorphins. The 5–15 minutes after orgasm are physiologically distinctive — deep relaxation, mild euphoria, warmth, lowered pain sensitivity, often a soft emotional openness. This is the "liking" phase in its purest form and it is almost universally rushed through.

- Stay lying down. Breathe slowly. Eyes closed.
- Continue very light touch — chest, belly, thighs — not the penis (which is usually hypersensitive).
- Notice the body. Where is the warmth? The heaviness?
- Let any emotion be. Some men feel a wave of tenderness, sometimes sadness, sometimes laughter. All normal.
- Five minutes minimum. Ten is better.

Men who add afterglow time report that the *whole session* feels more satisfying — because the session's last memory is deep contentment rather than cleanup.

## 11.8 Whole-body arousal

"Full-body orgasm" is a phrase used loosely. What men who report it describe is: arousal sensation that is not confined to the genitals but spreads through the pelvis, abdomen, chest, limbs; orgasm felt as waves through the whole body rather than pulses in the penis; sometimes trembling, sometimes involuntary movement, sometimes emotional release. It's real, it's learnable, and it's built from components already covered:

1. **Deep relaxation** of the non-pelvic body at high arousal (Chapter 8.8).
2. **Breath** — slow, deep, into the belly, with the exhale directed (in imagination) up the spine or out through the limbs. Tantric and Taoist traditions use elaborate versions of this; the effective core is simply "breathe fully, and put attention on sensation moving outward from the pelvis."
3. **Attention widening** (11.6) — from the frenulum to the whole body.
4. **Multiple zones** — nipples and perineum especially, which give the nervous system "evidence" that arousal is a whole-body event.
5. **Movement** — pelvic rocking, spinal undulation.
6. **Sustained plateau** at 8–9 for 15+ minutes. Whole-body arousal develops in time; it doesn't happen in a 5-minute session.
7. **Sound.**

The experience typically emerges gradually over weeks of practice, not in a single session. Early signs: tingling in the hands or feet at high arousal; warmth spreading up the abdomen; involuntary trembling in the legs. These are the nervous system beginning to route arousal more widely. Follow them.

## 11.9 Session design: putting it together

Three templates. Adapt.

### The 15-minute focused session

For when time is short but you want quality, not a reflex.

- 2 min: transition (breath, body scan, warm the lube).
- 3 min: non-genital + scrotum + perineum, slow.
- 6 min: penis, slow, lubricated, changing grip every minute; one hand on a secondary zone throughout. Get to 8. Two edge cycles.
- 2 min: approach and orgasm. Choose the finish. Exhale through it.
- 2 min: afterglow. Stay.

### The 45-minute full session

- 3 min: transition.
- 5 min: teasing. No penis contact. Thighs, abdomen, chest, nipples, scrotum, perineum. Approach and retreat.
- 10 min: build to 7–8 with maximum variety. Three receptor types (stroke, temperature, stretch or vibration). Change technique every 60–90 seconds. Eyes closed from 7.
- 15–20 min: plateau. Continuous edging in the 8–9 band. Reverse Kegel at each peak. Two-zone stimulation throughout. Breath and sound. Attention narrow-wide. Wave-riding. Let the body move.
- 3 min: approach. Settle into one technique and go deep. Slow down. Choose the pelvic floor strategy and the stimulation-at-orgasm strategy.
- Orgasm. Exhale. Present.
- 7 min: afterglow. Stillness. Light touch. Notice.

### The 90-minute deep session

For a planned occasion, after 2–4 days of anticipation.

- 5 min: shower, warm room, scent, dim light. Transition ritual.
- 10 min: body scan, breath. Then whole-body non-genital touch. Slow, light, caress-speed (C-tactile). Nipples introduced.
- 15 min: genital tease. Scrotum, perineum, external anal, base of shaft. Glans forbidden. Build to 6–7 this way.
- 10 min: prostate warm-up if practiced (Chapter 12); or vibrator on frenulum at low intensity with a hand on the perineum; or temperature play.
- 30 min: plateau. Everything from the 45-minute session, extended. Sleeve or toy for part of it to rest the hands. Position changes. Long waves. Whole-body attention.
- 5 min: approach. Deep on one technique. Perineal or prostate pressure ready.
- Orgasm, with everything: perineal pressure timed to the contractions, long exhale with sound, chosen pelvic floor strategy, full attention.
- 15 min: afterglow. Blanket. Stillness. Optionally, the non-ejaculatory or second-orgasm practices from Chapter 12.

## 11.10 Tracking and iterating

Pleasure is trainable, and what's trained is what's tracked. After each notable session, a 60-second journal entry (Chapter 21 template):

- Duration, structure, techniques, zones.
- Peak arousal reached and held (how long at 8–9?).
- Edge cycles.
- Orgasm rating (1–10), duration (approximate contraction count if you can), quality (sharp/rolling/whole-body).
- What was new. What worked. What didn't.
- Body state going in (sleep, time since last, mood).

After a month you'll see patterns — which inputs move your orgasm rating, which zones you've undervalued, what session length is your sweet spot. That's mastery: knowing your own instrument.

## 11.11 A note on diminishing returns and balance

The techniques here can make solo sex extraordinarily pleasurable. Two cautions:

1. **Don't make every session a production.** If only 90-minute engineered sessions satisfy you, you've moved the baseline in a way that makes ordinary sex — solo or partnered — feel inadequate. This is a form of the conditioning problem (Chapter 16). Keep quick sessions, medium sessions, and lazy sessions in rotation. The point of skill is *range*, not escalation.
2. **Pleasure engineering and compulsivity share a mechanism.** Anticipation, novelty-seeking, and extended wanting are exactly what drives "gooning" (Chapter 15). The difference is agency and consequence: are you choosing this, and is your life better for it? Check in honestly.

---

### Key takeaways

- Pleasure is the sum of **six dials**: anticipation, sensation, arousal state, attention, novelty, orgasm mechanics. Most men leave all six at default.
- **Wanting** (dopamine, build-up) and **liking** (opioids, orgasm/afterglow) are separate systems. The build-up is itself a main source of pleasure — extend it.
- **Anticipation**: plan sessions, abstain 2–4 days for peak intensity, tease before genital contact, edge.
- **Sensation**: add zones (scrotum, perineum, nipples, anus, prostate); recruit ≥3 receptor types (stroke, pressure, stretch, vibration, temperature, texture, caress-speed light touch); layer two zones at once.
- **Arousal state**: warm, rested, hydrated, 2–4 days since last, parasympathetic, morning or post-exercise.
- **Novelty**: change technique every 60–90 seconds during the build; vary sessions; break your "finishing move." **Novelty in the build, depth at the peak.**
- **Attention**: eyes closed at 8+, narrow-wide oscillation, breath, sound, movement, no clock.
- **Orgasm mechanics**: longer plateau (up to ~45–60 min), 5–10 edge cycles, chosen pelvic floor strategy, perineal pressure timed to contractions, long exhale with sound, stay present.
- **Afterglow**: 5–10 minutes minimum. This is where the liking system lives.
- **Whole-body arousal** develops over weeks from relaxation + breath + attention widening + multiple zones + movement + sustained plateau.
- **Track and iterate.** Keep range; avoid making every session a production.

# Chapter 12 — Advanced Sensations: Prostate, Perineum, Non-Ejaculatory & Multiple Orgasms

This chapter covers the practices that extend male sexual experience beyond the standard penile-stroke-to-ejaculation pattern: prostate stimulation, non-ejaculatory orgasm, multiple orgasms, and related techniques. It also covers a few practices (urethral sounding, extreme constriction) that circulate online and carry real risk.

Evidence quality here is lower than in earlier chapters. Prostate orgasms and male multiple orgasms are documented in clinical case literature and sexology surveys but have been studied far less than the standard response cycle. Much of what's known comes from practitioner communities. We flag the evidence level throughout and separate "well-documented" from "widely reported" from "speculative."

## 12.1 The prostate

### What's known

- The prostate is richly innervated by the pelvic plexus and responds to pressure with sensation ranging from neutral to intensely pleasurable [Levin 2018]. 🟢
- Prostate stimulation can produce orgasm, with or without ejaculation. Levin's review describes prostate-induced orgasms as reported to be "more intense, more diffuse, longer-lasting, and associated with greater feelings of ecstasy" than penile orgasms, and notes they can occur repeatedly without a refractory period. 🟡 (clinical reviews and surveys; no controlled physiological studies)
- Most men report no pleasure — or discomfort — from prostate stimulation *at first*, and increasing pleasure with repeated practice. Levin proposes a learning/neuroplastic mechanism: the brain learns to interpret the novel signal as sexual. 🟡
- The time to develop prostate pleasure varies enormously: some men within a few sessions, others after months, some never. 🟢 (consistent across all sources)
- Prostate stimulation does not indicate anything about sexual orientation. The prostate is innervated identically regardless of who you're attracted to. 🟢

### Anatomy recap

5–7 cm (2–3 inches) inside the rectum, toward the front of the body (toward the pubic bone). Walnut-sized, firm but yielding, with a shallow groove down the middle. When aroused, it swells and becomes easier to locate. The seminal vesicles are just above and behind it. The membranous urethra runs just below it — pressure there produces a distinct "need to urinate" or "about to ejaculate" sensation.

### Prerequisites

- Chapter 5.7 (anal hygiene and safety) — read it.
- Trimmed nails or a glove/finger cot. Lots of lube (low-osmolality water-based or silicone). Never numbing lube.
- Arousal first. Get to 6–7 with penile stimulation before any anal touch. The anus relaxes with arousal; the prostate engorges with arousal; and the brain is primed to read new signals as sexual.
- Relaxation. The external sphincter needs time. Slow breath, reverse Kegels.
- Patience across sessions. This is a weeks-to-months project, not a single-session goal.

### Progression

**Stage 1 — External (several sessions).** Lubricated fingertip circling the anal opening while stroking the penis. Light pressure on the opening without entering. Perineal pressure. The goal: the nervous system learns that this area is part of sex. Many men find this alone adds a distinct layer and stop here. That's fine.

**Stage 2 — Entry (several sessions).** At arousal 7, with a heavily lubricated finger, press gently against the opening and wait — don't push. On an exhale and reverse Kegel, the sphincter opens slightly; let the finger slide in to the first knuckle. Hold still. Breathe. When comfortable, further. Continue penile stimulation throughout (or pause and resume — some men find combining them overwhelming at first). The goal: comfort with the sensation of fullness and the sphincter stretch. Don't seek the prostate yet.

**Stage 3 — Locating.** Finger in to the second knuckle or beyond, curling toward the front (toward the pubic bone, as if pointing at the base of the penis from inside). You'll feel a firm, rounded bulge — the prostate — through the rectal wall. Press gently. The sensation at first is often odd: a dull pressure, a need-to-urinate feeling, maybe nothing. That's expected. Gentle circular pressure, or a slow "come here" beckoning motion with the fingertip.

**Stage 4 — Combining.** Prostate pressure while stroking the penis. The most common breakthrough: the prostate sensation that felt "odd" alone becomes distinctly pleasurable when penile arousal is at 8+. Press firmly on the prostate at the edge, and during orgasm. Many men who felt nothing from the prostate alone discover that prostate pressure *during* a penile orgasm dramatically intensifies it. This is the reliable, accessible version of prostate pleasure, and for many men it's the destination.

**Stage 5 — Prostate-focused (weeks to months).** Reduce penile stimulation; let prostate stimulation carry more of the arousal. A toy (Chapter 13) is much easier than a finger for this — fingers tire, angles are awkward, and a well-shaped prostate massager held in place by the pelvic floor can be worked hands-free. Contract and release the pelvic floor around the toy rhythmically. Small rocking movements of the hips. Breath. Attention entirely on the internal sensation. The commonly reported experience is a slow build over 20–60 minutes: warmth, fullness, pleasant pressure, then waves of sensation that feel like the *beginning* of an orgasm that don't peak in the usual way, then, with practice, waves that do peak — the "prostate orgasm" — without ejaculation and without refractory period. Some men reach this in a few weeks; many take months; some never do, and enjoy the intermediate states.

### What a prostate orgasm is like (as reported)

Compiled from clinical descriptions and community reports; individual experience varies widely:

- Builds slowly rather than sharply; often no clear "point of no return."
- Felt deep in the pelvis and spreading outward, rather than in the penis.
- Often described as waves — sometimes several minutes of rolling peaks.
- Frequently involves involuntary muscle contractions, trembling, vocalization.
- Erection may be present or absent; it's not required.
- Usually no ejaculation, though prostatic fluid may leak ("milking"). Some men ejaculate.
- Little or no refractory period; can be repeated or followed by a penile orgasm.
- Many men describe it as more emotional or "whole-body" than penile orgasm.

### Troubleshooting

- **"I feel nothing."** Normal for weeks. Keep combining with penile arousal at 8+. Try more pressure (the prostate tolerates firm pressure), or less. Try a toy with a different shape.
- **"It just feels like I need to pee."** You're pressing on the urethra/bladder area — aim slightly higher or more to the side. Also, empty the bladder beforehand. The sensation usually transforms with continued practice.
- **"It's uncomfortable."** Slow down. More lube. More arousal before entry. Smaller toy. Never push through pain.
- **"I lose my erection."** Common and irrelevant to prostate pleasure. Some men's erections soften during anal play; the pleasure is separate.
- **"I keep tensing up."** Reverse Kegels. Long exhales. Try a toy instead of a finger so both hands and attention are free.
- **"I got close but didn't peak."** The characteristic plateau. Keep practicing. Trying to force it inhibits it (prefrontal effect). Let it build without goal.
- **"There's a small amount of clear fluid."** Prostatic fluid expressed by pressure. Normal.
- **"Blood."** Stop. Minor fissure; wait until healed. Persistent bleeding: doctor.

### Milking

"Prostate milking" is the expression of prostatic fluid via massage, with or without orgasm. Historically used medically for prostatitis (efficacy debated). As a sexual practice it overlaps with the above. Not a health intervention.

## 12.2 The perineum and the "external prostate"

Firm upward pressure midway between scrotum and anus compresses the bulb of the penis, the membranous urethra, and reaches toward the prostate. This is accessible to everyone, requires nothing, and reliably intensifies orgasm for most men who try it.

- **During plateau:** rhythmic pressure with fingertips or knuckles, synchronized with strokes.
- **At the edge:** firm sustained pressure can either drop arousal (by interrupting the ejaculatory reflex) or spike it — depends on the man. Learn which for you.
- **During orgasm:** pressure in time with the contractions. The most common single technique for intensifying penile orgasm.
- **The "hold-back" (not recommended):** pressing very hard on the perineum at ejaculation to block semen from exiting. It works — semen is forced back into the bladder (retrograde ejaculation) — and the orgasm continues without visible ejaculate. It's a Taoist "injaculation" practice. Risks: retrograde ejaculation can, with repeated practice, become habitual; forceful pressure on the membranous urethra can cause irritation or, rarely, injury; and there's no evidence of the claimed "energy conservation" benefits. Occasional use is unlikely to harm; regular use is not advised.

## 12.3 Non-ejaculatory orgasm (NEO) and multiple orgasms

### What's known

- Orgasm and ejaculation are neurologically separable (Chapter 3). 🟢
- Men can learn to orgasm without ejaculating; this is reported in sexological literature since Kinsey and documented in case reports and surveys [Dunn & Trost 1989; Whipple, Myers & Komisaruk 1998 — a case study of a man with verified multiple orgasms]. 🟡
- Non-ejaculatory orgasm produces little or no refractory period, permitting multiple orgasms in a session. 🟡
- The proportion of men who can learn this is unknown. Practitioner communities suggest most men can learn *some* version with months of practice; a minority learn it quickly; some never do. 🟠
- Prolactin, the prime refractory-period suspect, surges after *ejaculation*; it's unclear whether it surges after non-ejaculatory orgasm. Small studies suggest less or none [Krüger 2003, indirectly]. 🟠

### Three routes

**Route 1 — The prostate route.** Described above. Prostate orgasms are typically non-ejaculatory by nature. This is the most reliable route for men who develop prostate pleasure.

**Route 2 — The edging route (the "dry orgasm" or "mini-orgasm").** With extensive edging practice, many men begin to experience, at 9–9.5, a brief wave of orgasmic sensation *without* tipping into ejaculation — a few involuntary pelvic contractions, a rush, and then arousal drops to 8 rather than to zero. This is often called a mini-orgasm, dry orgasm, or "peak." It emerges naturally from long-term edging in many men; it's not forced. Techniques that encourage it:

- Hold at 9 (not 9.5) longer and longer, with complete body relaxation and slow breath.
- At the first sign of involuntary pelvic pulses, *stop all stimulation* and *relax the pelvic floor completely* (reverse Kegel). Do not clench. Let whatever happens happen.
- Sometimes the result is a ruined orgasm (ejaculation, no peak); sometimes nothing; sometimes — increasingly, with practice — a peak with no ejaculation. The distinction is where on the 9–9.5 boundary you were.
- Once a dry orgasm happens, arousal drops to 7–8; resume stimulation; build again; another peak may be available. Sequences of 3–10 peaks over a session are reported by experienced practitioners. 🟠

**Route 3 — The clench (the "Kegel block").** At 9.5 — the point of no return, as emission begins — a *maximum-force sustained pelvic floor contraction* held for 10–15 seconds. Orgasm proceeds (the contractions are felt), but the strong voluntary contraction of the bulbospongiosus and external urethral sphincter partially or fully blocks expulsion. Semen may be retained in the urethra (and dribble out later), may go retrograde into the bladder, or may be partially expelled. Results reported: orgasm sensation usually present, sometimes muted; refractory period reduced but often not eliminated (since emission occurred); second orgasm possible within minutes. This is the technique in *The Multi-Orgasmic Man* (Chia & Arava 1996) and many online guides. 🟠

**Caveats for Route 3:** it requires a strong, well-trained pelvic floor (Chapter 10, several weeks in); timing is very precise and mistimed attempts either don't block anything or produce a ruined orgasm; frequent retrograde ejaculation may cause some men bladder irritation; and the technique tends to produce a "blocked" or "incomplete" sensation for many men rather than the "full orgasm, no ejaculation" it promises. Routes 1 and 2 produce more satisfying non-ejaculatory orgasms according to most practitioners. Route 3 is included because it's widely taught, it does work for some men, and knowing its limitations is useful.

### Multiple ejaculatory orgasms

Separately: some men, especially younger, can ejaculate a second (or third) time within minutes. The refractory period is short or absent for them at high arousal. Continued or renewed stimulation immediately after the first orgasm — through the hypersensitive phase, which is uncomfortable for many — leads to a second ejaculation, typically smaller and less intense. This is not learnable in the same way; it's largely constitutional and declines with age. If it's available to you, enjoy it; if not, the non-ejaculatory routes are the trainable path to multiples.

### A realistic timeline

- **Weeks 1–4:** edging proficiency (Chapter 9); pelvic floor foundations (Chapter 10). Nothing else yet.
- **Months 2–3:** holding at 9 for extended periods; first mini-peaks may appear; prostate exploration if interested.
- **Months 3–6:** dry orgasms with some reliability for those in whom they develop; prostate pleasure developing.
- **Months 6–12+:** multiple non-ejaculatory orgasms per session for some; prostate orgasms for some.

Some men are much faster; some plateau. Treat it as an interesting practice with pleasant intermediate states, not a pass/fail.

## 12.4 Combining everything: the extended-orgasm session

For men who've developed the components:

1. Standard build and edging (Chapter 9, 11) to 8–9.
2. Prostate toy in place from arousal 7.
3. At each edge: reverse Kegel, full relaxation, breath. Allow dry peaks if they come. Arousal drops to 8; continue.
4. Prostate stimulation carries arousal during pauses in penile touch.
5. Over 30–60 minutes: a series of peaks, waves, and prostate sensations.
6. Finish with an ejaculatory orgasm with perineal/prostate pressure — reported as exceptionally intense after this kind of session — or don't, and let the session end without ejaculation.

## 12.5 Other practices: what circulates online, and the risks

### Urethral sounding

Inserting a smooth rod ("sound") into the urethra. Real practice with a real following. Real risks: urethral tears, infection (UTI, and introduction of bacteria into the prostate — prostatitis), urethral stricture (scarring that permanently narrows the urethra, affecting urination and ejaculation), and — with objects not designed for it — foreign bodies requiring surgical removal. If you're going to do it despite this: only surgical stainless steel or medical silicone sounds designed for the purpose; sterilized (not just washed); sterile lube; never force; never past resistance; stop at any pain or bleeding; never with anything that can break or be lost. Urologists see the failures. We don't recommend it; we'd rather you know the harm-reduction facts than not.

### Extreme constriction / prolonged rings

Rings are covered in Chapter 13 — 20–30 minutes maximum, quick-release or stretch, remove at any pain, numbness, cold, or color change. "Sleeping with a ring on," rigid metal rings that get stuck, or multiple stacked rings for hours are how men end up in emergency departments having rings cut off with power tools. Ischemic damage can be permanent.

### Vacuum pumps

Legitimate medical devices (for ED) and legitimate pleasure devices, with a real risk profile if misused: bruising, petechiae, lymphatic swelling, numbness, and with extreme or prolonged vacuum, tissue damage. Use a pump with a pressure gauge and release valve; stay well under the maximum; 10–15 minutes per session; stop at pain. "Pumping" for size increase produces temporary swelling (edema), not growth, and repeated over-pumping damages the erectile tissue.

### Ball stretching, weights, and testicular pressure

Some men enjoy gentle downward traction on the scrotum (it's a legitimate sensation and a delay technique). Hanging weights, prolonged stretching devices, and forceful compression risk testicular trauma, torsion, and nerve damage. Gentle, brief, hand-only traction is fine. Devices are not recommended.

### Electrostimulation (e-stim)

Devices designed for the purpose (not TENS units repurposed, and never anything above the waist, which risks cardiac arrhythmia) deliver current through electrodes on the penis, perineum, or via insertable probes. Enthusiasts report unique sensations and hands-free orgasms. Use only purpose-built devices from reputable makers, follow instructions exactly, never near the heart, never if you have a pacemaker or heart condition, and start at minimum settings.

### Breath play, choking, and "autoerotic asphyxiation"

**Do not.** This kills several hundred people per year. There's no safe way to do it alone; the mechanism (hypoxia) is the same one that causes unconsciousness, and unconsciousness while alone with a ligature or bag is fatal. Any "safety" device or method can fail. This is the one practice in this guide with a flat prohibition rather than harm reduction.

### Chemsex / substance-enhanced solo sex

Chapter 18 covers substances. Briefly: alcohol impairs; cannabis at low doses enhances for many and impairs at higher doses; poppers (alkyl nitrites) relax smooth muscle including the anal sphincter and produce a brief rush, with real risks (blood pressure drop, dangerous with PDE5 inhibitors, chemical burns if spilled, and a form of retinal damage with some formulations); stimulants (meth, cocaine, MDMA) produce intense wanting with impaired liking and function, high compulsivity risk, and serious harms. Not recommended.

## 12.6 Evidence summary

| Practice | Evidence for pleasure | Evidence for safety | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| Prostate stimulation | 🟡 clinical + survey | 🟢 safe with basic precautions | Recommended to explore |
| Perineal pressure | 🟡 widely reported | 🟢 | Recommended |
| Perineal hold-back (injaculation) | 🟠 | 🟡 occasional OK; regular not advised | Occasional only |
| Non-ejaculatory orgasm via edging | 🟡 case reports + practitioner | 🟢 | Recommended as long-term practice |
| Non-ejaculatory via clench | 🟠 | 🟡 | Try with caveats |
| Prostate orgasm | 🟡 clinical review [Levin 2018] | 🟢 | Recommended to explore, patiently |
| Urethral sounding | 🟠 | 🔴 significant risk | Not recommended; harm reduction if you do |
| Constriction >30 min | — | 🔴 | No |
| Vacuum pumping (moderate) | 🟠 | 🟡 | Gauge, limits, caution |
| Ball weights/devices | 🟠 | 🔴 | No |
| E-stim (purpose-built) | 🟠 | 🟡 below the waist only | Caution |
| Breath play / asphyxiation | — | 🔴 lethal | **Never** |

---

### Key takeaways

- The **prostate** is a legitimate, learnable, orientation-irrelevant source of intense pleasure. It typically takes weeks to months to develop; start with external touch, combine with penile arousal at 8+, then progress to focused stimulation with a toy. Most men's first breakthrough is prostate pressure *during* a penile orgasm.
- **Perineal pressure** timed to orgasmic contractions is the simplest reliable intensifier.
- **Non-ejaculatory orgasm** is real and has three routes: prostate, edging-derived dry peaks, and the clench. The first two produce better results for most men. Expect months, not sessions.
- **Multiple orgasms** follow from non-ejaculatory orgasms (little refractory period).
- **Sounding, prolonged constriction, ball weights, over-pumping** carry real injury risk. **Breath play alone is lethal.** No exceptions.
